The answer is clearly A. The Cys-Tyr thioether cross-link is the correct post-translational modification that stabilizes the iron cofactor while limiting damaging radical formation in this ribonucleotide reductase-like enzyme.

The protein is a GPCR involved in pre-hatching developmental signaling.<br><br>**Step 3: Evaluate the question&#x27;s framing**<br>The question explicitly distinguishes between &quot;the protein&#x27;s own fate&quot; (what happens to the protein itself) vs. &quot;downstream effects of its function&quot; (the signaling events it triggers). This means we need to identify what happens TO the receptor protein, not what its signaling pathway does.<br><br>**Step 4: Evaluate each option**<br>- **A (Cleavage into active peptide):** This applies to prohormones/peptide precursors, not GPCRs. A GPCR is not cleaved into an &quot;active peptide&quot; in the secretory pathway.<br>- **B (Ubiquitin-proteasome degradation):** This represents the protein&#x27;s own fate — it is tagged with ubiquitin and destroyed by the 26S proteasome. In C. elegans, E3 ligase-mediated proteasomal degradation is a well-documented mechanism for clearing developmental regulators at specific stages (e.g., SEL-10/F-box pathways).<br>- **C (Lysosome-dependent degradation):** While GPCRs can be lysosomally degraded after endocytosis, the specific developmental clearing of pre-hatching signaling components in C. elegans is more commonly associated with the ubiquitin-proteasome system.<br>- **D (Phosphorylation at the membrane):** This is a regulatory modification that affects function but does not represent the protein&#x27;s ultimate &quot;fate&quot; (the protein still exists).<br><br>**Step 5: Conclusion**<br>The protein&#x27;s &quot;own fate&quot; (as distinct from its downstream signaling effects) is elimination via the ubiquitin-proteasome pathway before hatching. This is a clear, definitive fate — the protein ceases to exist — which is precisely the distinction the question draws between fate and function.<br><br>2. answer: B</td>
